|Ch49|p.1160|数据|Drying energy share & efficiency|Thermal drying is an energy-intensive operation that accounts for up to 15% of all industrial energy usage. Moreover, conventional dryers often operate at low thermal efficiency, typically between 25 and 50% but which may be as low as 10% also|★★★★☆｜Energy/cost lever on any airlaid/coating/pulp dryer|
|Ch49|p.1160|论断|Dryer control least-studied|The control of dryers is probably one of the least-studied areas of process control and has not progressed concurrently with improvements in drying and dryer design|★★★★☆｜Explains why off-the-shelf dryer control is weak; room for spec upgrades|
|Ch49|p.1160|论断|No reliable online moisture sensor|The lack of a direct, online, reliable method for sensing product moisture content|★★★★★｜The root cause of indirect, poor control on hygiene lines|
|Ch49|p.1161|论断|Moisture is the right output|The most desirable drying process output variable to control is the product moisture content, but this is often difficult to measure directly|★★★★★｜Residual moisture = the hygiene spec; this is the target variable|
|Ch49|p.1161|论断|Moisture inferred from exhaust T/H|the moisture content of the dried product can be inferred from the temperature and humidity of the exhaust gas, though care must be taken in applying appropriate heat and mass balances|★★★★★｜Basis for inferential control where direct sensing unavailable|
|Ch49|p.1161|数据|Moisture analyzer types|Analyzers are available for moisture analysis ... rely on infrared, microwave, or capacitance detection|★★★★★｜The sensor technologies to demand for a real moisture loop|
|Ch49|p.1162|论断|Indirect control poor correlation|due to the low correlation between the temperature and the actual product moisture content, using indirect control (by parameters such as temperature and humidity) usually results in poor control|★★★★★｜Temperature loop alone cannot hold a moisture spec|
|Ch49|p.1162|论断|Exhaust-T-alone can fail|In some special cases, this may even lead to serious failure if the exhaust temperature is used in isolation (without concurrent measurement of humidity)|★★★★★｜Single-instrument failure mode to forbid on tight-spec webs|
|Ch49|p.1162|数据|Common disturbances|The most common dryer disturbances are: 1. Ambient air temperature 2. Ambient air humidity 3. Feed moisture content 4. Feed composition|★★★★★｜The load variables a hygiene-line controller must reject|
|Ch49|p.1162|数据|Manipulated variables|1. Heating rate ... 2. Solids feed rate ... 3. Airflow rate (for direct dryers) 4. Rotational speed (for rotary dryers)|★★★★★｜The actuators available to hold moisture on a running line|
|Ch49|p.1163|公式|PID controller equation|m(t) = Kc e(t) + (Kc/tI) integral e(t)dt + Kc tD de/dt + BIAS [Eq 49.1; OCR garbled source]|★★★★☆｜Core feedback law; derivative often undesirable due to noise|
|Ch49|p.1163|论断|Dead time defeats feedback|If this dead time is large, it may result in inadequate performance of the feedback controller. To overcome this problem, a predictive type of control known as feedforward control is used|★★★★★｜Pulp/floater/Yankee dryers have dead time; need feedforward/MBC|
|Ch49|p.1164|数据|Table 49.1 FF vs FB tradeoffs|Feedforward: good for slow systems or those with significant dead time; Feedback: unsatisfactory for slow processes or those with significant dead time|★★★★★｜Selection rule that favours feedforward on long-residence dryers|
|Ch49|p.1164|论断|Inferential control definition|Inferential control ... is useful when the main dryer controlled variable (i.e., product moisture content) cannot be measured directly|★★★★★｜The practical route when no online moisture probe exists|
|Ch49|p.1165|论断|IMC robustness balance|IMC is advantageous because it can be adjusted to balance controller performance with control system robustness (when either modeling errors or changes in process dynamics occur)|★★★★☆｜Model-based route tolerates model error on variable feeds|
|Ch49|p.1166|论断|IMC responds rapidly no offset|If the system is performed without any oscillations, the overshoots will be tolerable, which there will be no offset, and the control scheme will be effective and respond rapidly|★★★★☆｜Demonstrated IMC benefit on fluid-bed drying|
|Ch49|p.1169|论断|Spray dryer safety interlocks|safety systems can shut down the air heater or pass water to nozzles ... Alarms might be added to detect the potentials for explosion, product plugging, flooding, high temperature, and loss of airflow|★★★★★｜Control must include explosion/feed-loss alarms (links Ch48)|
|Ch49|p.1170|公式|Shinskey adiabatic moisture Eq49.5|Xp = K ln[(Ti - Tw)/(To - Tw)] [Eq 49.5; Xp product moisture, Ti inlet-air T, To outlet-air T, Tw wet-bulb, K constant]|★★★★★｜Quantitative moisture-temperature link for flash/adiabatic dryers|
|Ch49|p.1171|论断|Fluid-bed reverse action|an increase in humidity of the entering air can cause a reduction in inlet-air temperature instead of increasing it|★★★★☆｜Humidity disturbance can mislead a T-only fluid-bed loop|
|Ch49|p.1172|论断|Temperature-difference method|the temperature difference between the material and the wet bulb is a function of the moisture content of the material|★★★★☆｜Infer moisture from material/wet-bulb DT where probes fail|
|Ch49|p.1173|论断|MBC beats PID under swings|MBC system functions better than either PID feedback control or lead/lag feedforward control ... able to avoid instabilities caused by the frequent and large variations in the feed moisture content without detuning|★★★★★｜Upgrade case for variable feed-moisture hygiene lines|
|Ch49|p.1173|数据|MBC held grain moisture ±0.5%|average outlet maize moisture content was controlled to ±0.5% of the set point|★★★★★｜Demonstrated achievable moisture control precision|
|Ch49|p.1174|论断|Drying is nonlinear/time-variant|The drying process is complex, time-variant, and nonlinear|★★★★☆｜Why classical PID alone is fragile on real hygiene lines|
|Ch49|p.1175|论断|Operator rules of thumb|The process operators' control strategy is based on anticipation, knowledge (know-how), and experience, and can be considered as a set of heuristic decision rules or 'rules of thumb.'|★★★★☆｜Basis for expert/fuzzy systems; captures tribal knowledge|
|Ch50|p.1181|论断|Dewatering+drying complementary|solid-liquid separation and drying are complementary operations in many industrial fields|★★★★★｜Mechanical dewatering is stage 1 of the drying system|
|Ch50|p.1183|论断|Balance dewatering cost vs dryer thermal|it is essential to balance the additional cost of producing a dry cake against the thermal requirements of dryers|★★★★★｜Optimisation frontier between press and dryer (links Ch46/Ch53)|
|Ch50|p.1183|论断|Fine cakes need expression stage|capillary pressures for 10-mm particles are so high ... limitation on moisture reduction ... unless an additional unit for mechanical expression was available|★★★★★｜Vacuum filter insufficient for fine pulp/SAP cakes; need expression|
|Ch50|p.1182|数据|Table 50.1 separation stages|Conditioning (flocculation/coagulation); Concentration (thickening/hydrocycloning); Separation (PVG/centrifuge); Posttreatment (washing/expression)|★★★★★｜Process map for pretreatment before the dryer|
|Ch50|p.1184|数据|Table 50.2 filter selection guide|Fast >1.0 cm/min >20% solids -> continuous vacuum/belt; Slow <0.12 cm/min -> pressure; Dilute -> thickening|★★★★★｜Right-size dewatering equipment to slurry rate|
|Ch50|p.1184|公式|Ruth filtration equation|dV/(A du) = p / [mu (alpha W/A + R_m)] (Eq 50.1)|★★★★☆｜Fundamental filtration-rate model|
|Ch50|p.1185|公式|Cake compressibility|alpha = alpha_0 p^n; n=0 rigid incompressible to 1.0 highly compressible; most slurries 0.1-0.8|★★★★★｜Determines whether higher pressure pays|
|Ch50|p.1185|公式|Incompressible cake rate|dV/(A du) = p / (mu alpha_0 W/A) (Eq 50.4); rate proportional to pressure|★★★★☆｜Pressure helps only for low-n cakes|
|Ch50|p.1185|公式|Compressible cake rate|dV/(A du) = 1 / (mu alpha_0 W/A) (Eq 50.5); rate independent of pressure for n=1|★★★★★｜Why high-pressure press may NOT help fine/compressible slurries|
|Ch50|p.1184|公式|Constant-pressure filtration|V^2/A^2 = K u (Eq 50.6); filtration time proportional to (cake thickness)^2|★★★★☆｜Batch filter cycle-time model|
|Ch50|p.1185|论断|Particle size vs cake moisture|Decreased particle size results in lower filtration rates and higher moisture content of the cake|★★★★★｜Fine pulp/SAP flocs hold more water; motivates conditioning|
|Ch50|p.1185|论断|Conditioning enlarges particles|flocculation/coagulation, polyelectrolytes, pH, temperature, freezing increase particle size -> easier separation|★★★★★｜Cheapest moisture cut is at the feed via conditioning|
|Ch50|p.1187|数据|Filter-press recess depth|depth of the recess is usually about 16 mm, giving a maximum cake thickness of about 32 mm|★★★★☆｜Mechanical limit on cake load per press cycle|
|Ch50|p.1187|论断|Filter press pros/cons|adaptability to high pressures, production of a dry washed cake; disadvantages high labor cost, leakage, air exposure|★★★★☆｜Trade-offs of plate-and-frame for hygiene streams|
|Ch50|p.1190|论断|Membrane press low moisture|a flexible membrane ... produces a cake with low residual moisture content|★★★★★｜Two-step filter+consolidation reaches low cake moisture|
|Ch50|p.1196|数据|Centrifuge siphon suction|level differential of 2 cm and centrifugal acceleration of 500g, a suction effect of 10-m column of water|★★★★☆｜Siphonic peeler centrifuge accelerates dewatering|
|Ch50|p.1196|论断|Batch centrifuge flexibility|duration and drum speed can be adjusted for each operation ... qualitative optimization|★★★★☆｜Batch centrifuge suits variable hygiene product parameters|
|Ch50|p.1196|论断|Continuous pusher centrifuges|single-stage and multi-stage rotors; slurry directed toward back of rotating bowl with reciprocating pusher plate|★★★★☆｜High-throughput continuous dewatering option|
|Ch50|p.1197|定义|Expression|separation of a liquid from a two-phase solid-liquid system by compression, due to movement of the retaining wall rather than pumping|★★★★★｜Defines mechanical squeeze vs filtration|
|Ch50|p.1197|数据|Expression pressure range|Expression pressures presently applied are between 0.6 and 2.5 MPa|★★★★★｜Operating pressure band for squeeze dewatering|
|Ch50|p.1197|公式|Expression time|time for expression is also proportional to the square of cake thickness|★★★★☆｜Scale-up rule for expression stage|
|Ch50|p.1197|论断|Two-step membrane filtration|first-step filtration under low pressure, followed by second-step filtration and cake consolidation under high expression pressure|★★★★★｜Optimal press cycle for low residual moisture|
|Ch50|p.1198|数据|Squeezer food/cleanliness use|good for applications in which cleanliness is crucial, as in food industries ... extraction of natural seasoning|★★★★☆｜Hygiene/cleanliness analogue for squeeze dewatering|
|Ch50|p.1199|论断|Equipment choice factors|choice of equipment depends on the objective of the separation, the properties of the slurry, and the scale of production|★★★★☆｜Decision basis for selecting dewatering tech|
|Ch50|p.1185|论断|Wash throughput = final filtration rate|rate of wash ... generally the same as the final rate of filtration (under same pressure, similar liquids, no rearrangement)|★★★★☆｜Cake-washing cost model for purity-critical streams|
|Ch51|p.1202|论断|Crystal size theme|The easy dewatering and drying of crystals requires that the crystals be of a uniform and sufficiently large size. This is, therefore, the major theme of this chapter|★★★｜Size/filterability/drying trade-off applies to any powder incl. SAP/actives|
|Ch51|p.1203|公式|Supersaturation definitions|Dc = c - c* (51.1); S = c/c* (51.2, S>1); s = (c-c*)/c* (51.3)|★★☆｜Supersaturation = undercooling analogue for polymer crystallization|
|Ch51|p.1203|论断|Supersaturation master variable|Supersaturation is the main parameter in determining the rate of crystal growth and nucleation ... determines the agglomeration, shape, and purity|★★★｜One knob governs all attributes; parallel to polymer crystallisation drivers|
|Ch51|p.1204|定义|Metastable zone|concentration is higher than in saturation but below the one where spontaneous nucleation occurs. Therefore, the crystal growth is mainly occurring in the metastable zone|★★★｜Metastable-zone width = nucleation-ease; direct analogue to polymer undercooling window|
|Ch51|p.1204|定义|Primary vs secondary nucleation|Primary nucleation ... homogeneous / heterogeneous; Secondary nucleation is defined as the generation of nuclei with the presence of crystals|★★★｜Nucleation taxonomy maps onto polymer spherulite nucleation|
|Ch51|p.1205|论断|Small crystals hard to dry/filter|the smaller the size of the crystals, the more difficult the dewatering and drying|★★★｜Fine SAP/actives fractions are hard to handle, filter and dry|
|Ch51|p.1205|公式|Population balance & mean size|@n/@t + G @n/@L = 0 (51.6); L50 = 3.67 G t (51.8); Ld = 3 G t (51.9)|★★☆｜CSD sizing relations; analogue to polymer crystalline-size control|
|Ch51|p.1205|论断|CSD prediction difficult|The prediction of CSDs is still quite difficult; hence, data on crystallization kinetics are required|★★☆｜Honest limit; chapter is qualitative guide not design manual|
|Ch51|p.1206|论断|Slow growth = purer crystal|As a general rule for purification by crystallization, the slower the crystal growth rate, the purer the crystal|★★☆｜Growth-rate/purity trade-off; analogue to residual monomer in polymers|
|Ch51|p.1206|论断|Mother-liquor inclusions lower purity|If agglomeration takes place, the secondary crystals may contain inclusions of the mother liquor, which decreases the purity|★★☆｜Trapped solvent/impurity analogue to polymer particle VOC residue|
|Ch51|p.1206|定义|Polymorphism|A chemical may have several crystal structures; the chemical compositions of these polymorphs are identical. This phenomenon is called polymorphism|★★★｜Solid-form concept parallel to polymer crystalline form (iPP isotactic etc.)|
|Ch51|p.1206|定义|Enantiotropy/Monotropy/Pseudopolymorph|reversible transformation = enantiotropy, irreversible = monotropy; Pseudopolymorphism occurs when a solvent enters the crystal lattice and forms a hydrate/solvate|★★☆｜Polymorph taxonomy; conceptual link to polymer crystalline/amorphous forms|
|Ch51|p.1208|数据|Seeding rules|0.1 to 0.3 times the final size and 0.1 to 3% of the final mass of the seed area are good choices ... seed crystals of 20 to 50 mm for final 500 to 1000 mm|★★☆｜Quantified seeding prevents runaway nucleation; analogue to nucleating agents|
|Ch51|p.1208|论断|Ostwald ripening of tiny seeds|small crystals tend to dissolve due to the Ostwald ripening effect and the crystal growth rates of tiny crystals are lower than that of larger ones|★★☆｜Size-dependent dissolution; relevant to fine-powder stability|
|Ch51|p.1210|论断|Fines destruction trade-off|very useful in increasing the size of crystals ... This method does, however, lead to a wide particle size distribution|★★★｜Bigger mean vs narrower distribution tension = granule-spec trade-off|
|Ch51|p.1216|公式|Kozeny-Carman filtration|If the particle diameter drops to 10% of its original value, the pressure drop increases by 100-fold. If viscosity increases tenfold, pressure drop increases tenfold|★★★｜Quantifies why sub-10um fractions cost 100x to filter|
|Ch51|p.1217|数据|Filter media synthetics|synthetic fibres, such as nylon, polyester, polypropylene, polyethylene, or fluorocarbon|★★☆｜Filter media are the same polymers used in hygiene nonwovens/films|
|Ch51|p.1217|数据|Table51.2 crystal sizes|Citric Acid medium 0.41mm; KCl 1.1mm; Na2SO4 0.30mm (FC/DTB/FC crystallizers)|★★☆｜Real CSD magnitudes for suspension crystallizers|
|Ch51|p.1218|论断|Drying window ~1mm|crystals be large, within the range of about 1 mm or above ... crystal sizes above approximately 1 mm tend to be harmful|★★★｜Concrete sizing band portable to SAP/actives granule specs|
|Ch51|p.1218|论断|Large crystals purer (same liquor thickness)|large crystal size also improves purity because the same thickness of the attached mother liquor on the surface ... results in a lower level of impurities|★★☆｜Surface-liquor purity lever; analogue to particle residual solvent|
|Ch51|p.1219|论断|Gentle dryers for crystals|both fluid-bed dryers and pneumatic dryers are relatively gentle, perhaps, because of the shorter residence time|★★☆｜Dryer-type guidance if a crystalline active is later dried|
|Ch51|p.1221|论断|Supersaturation selects polymorph|the level of supersaturation can be used to control the crystal shape ... Ma and Braatz showed supersaturation selected different polymorphs|★★☆｜Process-controlled solid form; conceptual only for hygiene actives|
|Ch52|p.1223|定义|Frying definition|Frying is defined as a process of cooking and drying through contact with hot oil ... a reduction in water activity at the surface or throughout the food|★☆☆☆☆｜Hot-oil food drying; mechanism unrelated to hygiene webs|
|Ch52|p.1225|论断|Heat-and-mass-transfer nature|a complex heat-and-mass-transfer phenomenon takes place. Heat is transferred by convection ... and by conduction within the solid food. Water is vaporized ... while oil penetrates into the food|★☆☆☆☆｜Generic HT/MT framing only; no web-specific content|
|Ch52|p.1225|论断|Four frying stages|composed of four distinct stages: 1. Initial heating ... 2. Surface boiling ... 3. Falling rate ... 4. Bubble end point|★☆☆☆☆｜Stage taxonomy not portable to dryer design|
|Ch52|p.1225|论断|Falling-rate period analogue|resembles the falling-rate period observed in food dehydration processes ... the crust layer increases in thickness|★★☆☆☆｜Falling-rate language is the only conceptual bridge to conventional drying|
|Ch52|p.1225|论断|Core pinned at boiling point|The moist core of the material cannot exceed the boiling point of the liquid phase|★☆☆☆☆｜Boiling-plateau fact; no design use for hygiene|
|Ch52|p.1227|数据|Crust/core interface temp|The crust/core interface remained at a constant temperature of 102°C after the crust was formed|★☆☆☆☆｜Specific to fried food; not a hygiene number|
|Ch52|p.1225|论断|Crust = moving moisture barrier|Oil penetrates into the food at the dried region that has a porous structure ... As more water leaves the food and the crust thickness increases, more oil is absorbed|★★★｜Developing surface layer throttles interior drying = case-hardening analogue for coated webs/cores|
|Ch52|p.1229|论断|Crust linked to oil penetration|Crust formation is a characteristic of fried foods and is closely linked to oil penetration|★★★｜Crust-as-barrier mechanism is the one transferable idea to over-dried hygiene substrates|
|Ch52|p.1234|论断|Case-hardening zone|food begins to case harden. When case hardening is complete food pieces have less tendency to stick together, but they are still pliable and malleable|★★★｜Case-hardening = surface skin seals interior; exact defect mode in aggressive web/core drying|
|Ch52|p.1234|论断|Crust thickness vs temperature|Crust thickness and subsurface structure depend greatly on frying temperature. Higher temperatures result in a thinner crust layer|★★★｜Temp/rate controls surface-barrier formation; parallels hot-surface case-hardening of cores|
|Ch52|p.1239|论断|Temp too low -> penetration before cook|Frying at temperatures that are too low allows too much fat to penetrate the crust before the interior is cooked|★★☆☆☆｜Rate-vs-penetration trade-off analogy for over-slow drying|
|Ch52|p.1229|公式|Oil uptake first-order|O(t) = O_e [1 - exp(-k t)]|★☆☆☆☆｜Empirical oil-absorption kinetics; no hygiene analogue|
|Ch52|p.1229|公式|Baumann-Escher oil correlation|F = C1 + C2 t + C3 T + C4 DM_r + C5 W + C6/b|★☆☆☆☆｜Fried-food oil model; irrelevant to fluff/SAP drying|
|Ch52|p.1228|数据|Oil absorbed during cooling|only 20% of the total oil ... was absorbed inside the chip during frying, while it amounted to 64% during cooling|★☆☆☆☆｜Capillary post-fry uptake; not relevant to hygiene lines|
|Ch52|p.1228|数据|Final moisture for shelf life|the moisture content of chips ... should be within 2 to 3% ... oil content should be as low as possible|★☆☆☆☆｜Drying-target spec for snacks; not a hygiene substrate|
|Ch52|p.1241|数据|Crispness loss vs moisture|Potato chips are fried to a final moisture level of 3% or less, which accounts to an a_w of about 0.2|★☆☆☆☆｜Water-activity shelf-life fact; no diaper relevance|
|Ch52|p.1232|论断|Oil viscosity rises with degradation|The viscosity of an oil is strongly affected by the degradation during frying, increasing as a result of the formation of dimers and polymers|★☆☆☆☆｜Polymer formation is oxidative degradation, not polymerization science|
|Ch52|p.1238|论断|Depth/absolute filtration|Depth filters may retain particles down to about 1 mm ... absolute filters ... retain particles down to 5 mm|★☆☆☆☆｜Filtration of frying oil; unit likely um by OCR - no hygiene use|
|Ch52|p.1226|公式|Chip conduction model|@Q/@t = a @2Q/@x2 (52.1)|★☆☆☆☆｜1-D conduction slab model; transport only, no substrate link|
|Ch53|p.1247|论断|Dryer is one component of system|A dryer itself is only one component of the overall drying system. The system may comprise preprocessing equipment ... gas cleaners ... and solvent-recovery systems|★★★★★｜Cost the whole battery-limits system, not just the dryer|
|Ch53|p.1246|公式|Inflation correction (M&S)|Updated cost / Original cost = M&S Index at updated time / M&S Index at the time of original cost|★★★★★｜Normalise any old/vendor quote to current dollars|
|Ch53|p.1246|数据|M&S and CE index history|M&S 1989 895.1 / 2004 1178.5; CE 1989 355.4 / 2004 444.2|★★★★☆｜Index magnitudes for cost updating|
|Ch53|p.1246|公式|Capacity scaling exponent|Predicted cost / Original cost = [desired capacity / original capacity]^n ... n = 0.6 can be taken for rough estimation|★★★★★｜Capacity^0.6 sub-unity scaling; right-sizing defence|
|Ch53|p.1247|数据|Table 53.2 equipment exponents|cyclone 0.61; cloth dust collector 0.78; venturi scrubber 0.46; roll crusher 0.97; centrifugal fan 0.44|★★★★★｜Device-specific scaling n for ancillary cost estimation|
|Ch53|p.1247|公式|Spray dryer cost equation|Price = 13793 · E^0.435 (first-quarter-1995 US$); E evaporative load 181<E<38780 kg/h|★★★★☆｜Direct spray-dryer capital correlation (detergent/ SAP analogue)|
|Ch53|p.1248|公式|Eq 53.3 dryer cost [OCR garbled]|C (M&S Index) = 30 + (17.77 T) + 36.74 Q [equation garbled in source]|★★★｜General cost correlation; source OCR-degraded, use Eq 53.5 instead|
|Ch53|p.1249|数据|Material multiplier|304-stainless steel typically costs from 1.5 to 1.7 times more than carbon steel and 2 to 3 times more if built of titanium|★★★★★｜Stainless/clad hygiene lines carry 50-70% equipment premium|
|Ch53|p.1249|论断|Do not use throughput as cost parameter|dryer cost depends mainly on its size and the material of construction, drying indices such as throughput, evaporation rate, or unit heat consumption should not be used as parameters for cost estimation|★★★★★｜Cost driver is size + material, not throughput|
|Ch53|p.1250|数据|Installed-cost factor|multiply the equipment FOB cost by a factor of 2.25 if carbon steel or by 2.75 if stainless-steel|★★★★★｜FOB-to-direct-cost rule; fastest system-cost sanity check|
|Ch53|p.1250|数据|Assembly/piping factor of FOB|Fully equipped compact system 25-30% of FOB; delivered in many parts 40-45% of FOB|★★★★☆｜Installation scope as % of equipment price|
|Ch53|p.1250|论断|FCI up to 3x equipment cost|FCI of a drying plant ... may be much higher, even by 3 times of the equipment cost|★★★★★｜Total capital dwarfs the dryer line item|
|Ch53|p.1250|数据|Building cost factor|inside a building 20-35% of installed cost; outside 15% of installed cost|★★★★☆｜Building envelope as % of installed cost|
|Ch53|p.1251|数据|Fixed-cost factor: depreciation|Depreciation of equipment 7 to 9% of installed cost|★★★★★｜Annual fixed-charge component|
|Ch53|p.1251|数据|Fixed-cost factor: interest|Interest charges on the investment 3 to 5% of FCI|★★★★★｜Financing cost component of FCI|
|Ch53|p.1251|数据|Fixed-cost factor: maintenance|Complex system 10% of FCI; Simple system 5% of FCI|★★★★☆｜Maintenance burden varies with system complexity|
|Ch53|p.1251|数据|Fixed-cost factor: overhead|Executive salaries and other fixed costs 6% of FCI; taxes/rent 2%; insurance 1%|★★★★☆｜Overhead/insurance/tax fixed charges|
|Ch53|p.1252|论断|Energy price is main constraint|The main constraint at this point is the cost of energy because of unstable prices of fuels|★★★★★｜Energy volatility dominates variable cost (links Ch46)|
|Ch53|p.1252|公式|Utility price model|P_utility = A·(CE Index) + B·P_fuel|★★★★☆｜Separates inflation and fuel-price utility terms|
|Ch53|p.1253|数据|Labor rule batch vs continuous|2 man-hour/(m3 dried material) + 1/3 man/dryer/shift (batch); 1/2 man/dryer/shift (continuous)|★★★★☆｜Labor-cost estimation rule for dryer selection|
|Ch53|p.1255|公式|Eq 53.5 worked spray dryer|P = 1221.7 × 47 × 570^0.52 = 1,554,000 (US$ 2006)|★★★★★｜Concrete equipment-cost calculation example|
|Ch53|p.1255|数据|Table 53.3 A,c (selected)|Spray A=47 c=0.52; Drum A=21 c=0.50; Belt conveyor A=21 c=0.59; Direct rotary A=14-17 c=0.66-0.68; Fluid bed batch A=141 c=0.47|★★★★★｜A,c coefficients to cost common hygiene-line dryers|
|Ch53|p.1256|数据|Worked FCI build-up|FOB 1,757,000 -> installed 2,632,000 -> direct 3,948,000 -> FCI 5,882,520|★★★★★｜End-to-end factor-method demonstration|
|Ch53|p.1256|数据|Fixed cost result|fixed cost for drying ... US$1,449,780 per annum ... US$41.09/t of dried product|★★★★★｜Annual fixed unit cost benchmark|
|Ch53|p.1256|数据|Variable cost result|Fuel 1,004,400; Electricity 86,184; Labor 473,040; Lost raw mat 57,240; Variable 1,685,699 = US$47.78/t|★★★★★｜Energy dominates variable cost; quantifies Ch46 savings entry|
|Ch53|p.1257|数据|All-in unit drying cost|US$41.09/t + US$47.78/t = US$88.87/t = US$0.089/kg|★★★★★｜Headline normalised unit-cost for supplier comparison|
|Ch53|p.1257|论断|Correct for inflation and geography|necessary to correct the cost estimate for the inflation index and also for different geographical locations|★★★★★｜Local cost of energy and regulations must be factored|
|Ch53|p.1257|论断|Estimate reliability +-30%|reliability of the estimate of +30% covers the majority of the cases tested|★★★★☆｜Set pessimistic band on preliminary FCI|
|Ch53|p.1253|论断|Compare on FCI and annual cost per production|relate the capital investment (FCI) and the annual drying costs to the annual production rate|★★★★★｜Normalise all bids to $/t dry product for comparison|
